Subj: Change of duty
Ref: (a) BUPERS PERS BLL5J desp 2618532 of APR 1951 :
1. Reference (a), certified original despatch, is transcribed herewith:
"when directed by your commanding officer, about 1 May, you will
regard yourself detached from duty at the U. S. Naval Air Station,
Barber's Point, T. He and from such other duty as may have been assigned;
will proceed to a port in which Commander, Fleet Air Activity, Japan-
Korean may be and upon arrival report for duty at the Photographic
Technical Office, Aviation Supply Branch, Yokosuka.
"Travel via government transportation is directed outside the :
United States. Class TWO priority is certified for travel via govern-
ment aircraft.
"Cost of this travel is chargeable to subhead 18, MPN current on
date of detachment (171153 for 1951 - 1721453 for 1952), expenditure
account 7/110, object class 029.
"Qualified 1255), 161270, 18200."
2. Immediately upon reporting for duty in compliance with these orders,
or any modifications thereto, you are directed to forward to the Chief
of Naval Personnel via your new commanding officer, a copy of these
orders complete with all endorsements thereto, including the final
; reporting endorsement (staff corps officers an additional copy to
cognizant Bureau).
3. Delivered this date.
T. RASSIEUR

duty status.
(b) BuPers Circular Letter 175""48.
1. Your application £of transfeF to connnissioned limited duty status
has been carefully considered by the Selection Board convened in April
1949 by the Secretary of the Navy. I regret to inform you that your ·
name was not included among those recommended for appointment. However•
I wish to assure you that this decision is no reflection upon your per•
formance of duty in the Navy; nor will it in any way affect your present
statuso
2o The number of applicants desiring transfer to commissioned limited
duty status has been far in excess of the number of appointments which
can be offeredo Therefore, it was necessa._ry for the Selectio~ Board~ - to recommend, fr om among eligible ·applicants, only thos e best qua.lit ~
for appointmento The decision in your case was made after a careful __....
review of your entire record and your qualificatio~ as compared with1
, those of other applicants of your_rank and classification or enlisted
ratinge
3. The interest you have sholln in pursuing a naval career represents
an exemplary attitude of service and patriotism. The Navy is sinc:erely
appreciative ot your contribution toward maintaining the naval eatab•
liabment at a high level of efficiency during the war, the demobiliza•
tion period• and the continuing period of world unrest.
T. L. SPRAGUE

2. In accordance with reference (a), when directed by the Commanding Officer,

from duty with Utility Squadron FIVE and from such other duty as may have been
assi~ed. You will proceed to a port in the United States and upon arrival
further proceed and report to the Commanding Officer, u. s. Naval Air Technical
Training Unit, Naval Air Station, Pensacola,Florida for duty.
3. You are hereby authorized to delay for a period of thirty (30) days, provided

Naval Personnel Manual.

From: Chief of Naval Personnel
To: CHPHOT Delbert C. NEWHOUSE, USN
Naval Supply Center
Oakland, Calif.
Via: Commanding Officer
Subj: Change of duty
1 . When directed by your commanding officer, on or about l June 1954, you
will regard yourself detached from duty at the Naval Supply Center, Oakland,
Calif., and ·from such other duty ar may have been assigned you; will proceed
to San Francisco, Calif., and report to the Commandant, Twelfth Naval District,
for transportation to the port in which the Commanding Officer, Utility
Squadron FIVE may be, and upon arrival report to the Commanding Officer,
Utility Squadron FIVE, for duty.
2. Provided no excess leave is involved, you a.re hereby authorized to delay
for a period of ten days in reporting at San Francisco, Calif.,
in compliance with these orders, such delay to count as leave.
3. Travel via government transportation is directed outside the United States.
Class TWO priority is certified for travel via government aircraft.
4. Cost of travel is chargeable to 1741453.18, MPN 1954, object class 029,
expenditure account 74120, Bureau Control 22/31600.
5,. Upon reporting, forward to BuPers a copy of these orders (Staff Corps Officers
an additional copy to cognizant Bureau) via new commanding officer with all endorse￾ments. Where leave or delay is involved, keep old and new station advised of
address using Form NavPers 3036.

From: The Chief of Naval Personnel.
Tog LT Delbert C. N' E W H O' U S E, USN, Office of Bureau of Aeronautics General Repre- sentative, central District,
Wright-Patterson Air Force Base,
Dayton, Ohio.
Via: Bureau of Aeronautics General Representative, Central Dis tric t .
Subject: Temporary additional duty.
1. On or about 5 July 1949, you will proceed to the following places
for temporary duty in connection with aeronautical matters:
Rochester, N. Y.,
Binghampton, N.Y. , Baltimore, Md.
2. You are authorized to omit or revisit any of the above mentioned
places and to vary the above itinerary as may be deemed necessary.
3. This is i n addition to your _pres ent duties and upon the completion
thereof you will return to Dayt·on, Ohio and resume your regular duties.
4. Travel via government aircraft is directed where available. Class
TWO priority is certified .
5. You will be reimbursed on a per diem basis while absent from your f
station in accordance with Par. 4l53, wr.r. ... .

INSTRUCTIONS
This form must be sworn to before an officer of the United States Navy or Marine Corps authorized to administer oaths, or before a notary public.
The full names and addresses of the beneficiaries must be stated care­ fully. If a married woman, her own given name should be stated; thus:
"Mrs. Anna May Smith," not "Mrs. John Smith."
This slip shall be made out and handled as follows:
Officer, Navy: Forward to Bureau of Naval Personnel.
Officer, Marine Corps: Forward to Commandant, Marine Corps. Nurse: Forward to Bureau of Medicine and Surgery.
New beneficiary slips shall be executed and forwarded as above in all cases of change in status of the grantor or in that of his or her bene•
ficiaries. In any event, payment will be made to the widow or children, if any whether designated or not, but not to a mother or other relative unles~
designated, U. S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE 10-32303-2

INSTRUCTIONS
This form must be sworn to before an officer of the
United States Navy authorized to administer oaths, or
before a notary public.
The full names and addresses of the beneficiaries must
be stated carefully. If a married woman, her own given
name should be stated: thus: "Mrs. Anna May Smith," not
"Mrs. John Smith."
This slip shall be made out and handled as follows:
Officer, Navy: Forward to Bureau of Naval Personnel.
Nurse: Forward to Bureau of Medicine and Surgery.
Enlisted men, Navy: In duplicate: one copy to Bureau
of Naval Personnel, one copy secured inside service rec­ ord. In cases of men entitled to money allowances for
quarters for enlisted men with dependents, t.1vo additional
copies, bearing the required certificate, to the Disbursing
Officer.
New beneficiary slips shall be executed and forwarded
as above in all cases of change in status of the granfor or
in that of his or her beneficiaries.
In any event, payment will be made to the widow or
children, if any, whether designated or not.
Normally casualty notification will be sent to all the
beneficiaries designat ed on this form. If, by reason of (
unusual circumstances, other special arrangements ore
desired, a statement should be attached setting forth
full instructions including full names, addresses, and
relationships.

1. Enclosure (1) is authority for the promotion of subject officer.
2. Prior to delivery of enclosure (1) t he commanding officer must
detennine that subject officer is mentally, morally and professionally
qualified to perform the duties of t he gr ade indicated therein, and
that he meets the physical qualifications prescribed below.
3. If subject officer' s health record indicates that he has undergone
an annual physical examination within the four months preceeding receipt
of this letter, and no disqualifying defects were noted, he is to be
considered physically qualified for promotion and the appointment may be
delivered.
4. If subject officer has not had an annual physical examination within
the above stated time, or if he has had an examination wherein disqualifying
defects were noted, the corru.ianding officer shall direct him to report to a
medical officer for physical examination for promotion. 'lbe physical stand￾ards shall be the same as those for retention on active duty. The findings
of the medical officer should read, "is (or is not) physically qualified for
continuation on active duty and for promotion to the grade of Chief Warrant
Officer W.4 "• Report of this physical examination will be. made to the Chief
of the Bureau of Medicine and Surgery on Standard Form 88.
~• In the event the commanding officer should ~e~ermine ~hat s~bject offi7er
is not mentally, morally or professionally qualified or, if he is not ph?si￾cally qualified for promotion, enclosure (1) shall be returne~ to the Chief
of Naval Personnel, Attn: Pers B1311, with a report of the circumstances.

Photographer in the U. s. Navy, to rank from - - - - - , do hereby accept such appointment, and do solemnly swear (or _1· ·
affirm) that I will support and defend the Constitution of the _ United States against all enemies, foreign and domestic; that - I will bear true faith and allegiance to the same, that I ~ake
this obligation freely, without any mental reservation or pur- pose of evasion; and ttet I will well and faithfully discharge the duties of the office on which I am about to enter. So help
me God.
Subscribed and sworn to before me
September 1946.

21 9S93
t 9 DEC194g From: Chief, Bureau of Aer onautics.
To: Chief .of Naval Personnel.
Via: Chief of Naval Operati ons (Op-,4).
Subj: LIEUTENANT DELBERT c. NEWHOUSE; information concerning termination
of temporary appointment.
1. Fon1arded for consideration.
2. During the period 27 August 1948, to 29 September 1949, whil e on
duty at the Bureau of Aeronautics Gener al Representative , Central Dis.a· r
triot Headquart ers, Wri ght-Patterson Air For ce Base, DaYton, Ohio, ·
Lieutenant Newhouse served as Bureau of Aer onautics liaison officer
with t he Air Materiel Command in matters relating to procurement of ' . ~
photographic items 1mder t he singl e-service purchase assignment which
was awarded to the Ai r Force by t he Depart ment of Defense.
J. On 10 August 1949, the Head of the Procurement Br anch of the
Photographic Divisi on was order ed to sea duty and it became necessary
t o obt ain a suitable r elief. Lieutenant Newhouse was ordered t o this
duty i n t he Bureau of Aeronautics because of his exceptional qualiff ca­ tions and recent experience in proouren:ent procedures gained while on
duty at Ai r Nateriel Command Headquarters, DaYt,on, Ohioo He is now
serving as Head of the Procurement Branch, Ph:tographic Division, during
which time his services have been enti rel y satisf actory-.
'~T. C.

6. Within my specialty I have been consistently ordered t o duty of
ever i ncreasi ng responsibility from Photographic Officer of Naval
Air Stations; through Photographic Liaison Off icer of Naval Aviati on

of the Bureau of Aeronautics.
7. While I appr eciate the many problems which were f aced by t he Burea~ of Naval Personnel and the Robinson Board in listing per sonnel to revert, it appears t hat reference (b), the governing directi ve, was not f ollowed

[Page 358]
DEPARTMENT OF THE NAVY
BUREAU OF AERONAUTICS
WASHINGTON 25, D. C. IN REPLY REFER TO
23 November 1949
From: Lieutenant Del bert c. Ne\7house, USN/177283/1350
'To: Chief, Bureau of Naval Personnel
Via: Chief, Bureau of Aeronautics
Subj: Information concerning termination of temporary appointment
Ref: (a) BuPers ltr 177283/1350 dtd 12 Oct 1949
(b) BuPers C/L No. 148-49 dtd 15 Sept 1949
Encl: (1) Transcripts of portions of representative fitness reports
1. I received reference (a) on 25 October 1949 notifying me that my
temporary appointment as lieutenant would terminate on 31 December 19490
2. Reference (b) published the policy for the priority.of reversion
of temporary officers and asserted that it was highly desirable t hat
r educt ions be effected on a logical basis, considering both the best
interests of t he service and the most equitabl e consideration of the
temporary officers concerned. Paragraph 2(d) stated that temporary
officers, permanent commissioned warrant, would be reverted in ascending
order of r etirement dat es.
3. Aft er r eceipt of reference (a), I have made a careful examination
of my correspondence and fitness report files in the Bureau of Naval
Personnel. Mat er ial of a possibly adverse nature was disclosed by
this examination in that an officer's fitness report dated 29 February
1948 was checked in the unfavorable box and was signed by Captain J.
C. Cronin, USN, then Commander Utility Wings, Pacific. A partial
transcript thereof is appended hereto as Exhibit "A" of Enclosure (1).
This was my first report from this command and covered the period from
23 October 1947 to 29 February 1948. I was transferred from the com￾mand on 1 July 1948 and a transcript of section 12 of my· next and final
fitness r eport from Captain Cronin is appended hereto as Exhibit 11Bn
of Enclosure (1).
4. The balance of all my fitness reports have ranged from above average
to superior and, almost without exception, have recommended retention
in temporary rank, transfer to regular commissioned rank, and promotion
when el igible. Exhibits "C" through "G" to Enclosure (1) are submitted
as being truly representative of my fitness as an officerQ

ENCLOSURE (ll
Exhibit "A" 29 February 1948 Captain J. c. Cronin, USN
12. 0 untavorable. a
Lt. Newhous e is a reserved, cheerful, and generally likeable officer.
He is having a most difficult time adjusting himself to staff duty.
He is judged compet ent i n t he technical aspects of photography, but
his per f ormance of his administrative duti es in both the photographic
and operati onal department s is below aver age. It is felt t hat his
deficiency is principally due to his background and lack of experience
in admini strat ion. He is making a sincere effort to correct this
def i ciency. He is not yet fitted f or pr omotion.
Exhibit aBn 1 July 1948 Captain J.C. Cronin, USN
12. "Satisfact ory."
Lt. Newhouse is a quiet, reserved, and likeable officer. He has had
a diff icult time adjusting himself to his duties on this staff, but
he has earnestly endeavored to do so. If hds experience in naval
aviation had been wider, it is believed he would have per formed his
duties bet ter. He has performed his technical duties as Photogr aphic
Officer well. His performance of admini strative duties, both as
Photogr aphic Officer and as Assistant Operations Officer, has improved
during the peri od of this report. He is not yet fit ted for promotion.
Exhibit "C" 19 August 1946 Admiral E. D. Foster,SC,USN
12. "Satisfactory.•
Lt. Newhouse has carried out his duties as Photogr aphic Liaison Officer
i n a manner which has r efl ected credi t on this Depot. He . has a confident
and quiet manner and excellent military conduct. His screening of
photographic r equests and handli ng of photogr aphic problems has dis￾played an initiative and thoroughness which is most desirable for an
officer in hi s parti cular billet. Lt. Newhouse is recommended for pr o­ motion when due and f or commissi oned rank in t he r egular Navy.

Exhibit 11D 11 13 March 1947 Cdr. K.A.Grahn,Jr.,SC,USN
12. 11Satisfactory.tt
Lt. Newhouse has shown a superior grasp of photographic supply details,
sound judgment, foresight, devotion to duty and thoroughness in his
primary duty as photographic officer . He carries out his depot duties
as well as liai son with, and assist ance to, ComAirPac and CinCPac with
tact and efficiency, and is firm i n administering photographic supply
doctrine in t hi s ar ea. As Administrati ve Officer he is in charge
of over 35 civilians and acts as division officer for 40 enlisted men,
performing both assignment ~ very well. His personal and military
characteri st i cs are the highest , he is r ecommended for promotion when
eligible.
Exhibit "E"
Rear Admiral C.W.Fox,SC,USN 23 May 1947 Cdr.E.R.Joshua,Jr.,SC,USN
12. "Sat isf actory.a
Lt. Newhouse possesses a sound grasp of supply procedures aqd has
exerted a beneficial infl uence in this area in carrying out instructions
of the Chief of Naval Operations, Chief, Naval Photogr aphic Services.
His judgment is sound and mature; he shows consistent devotion to duty
and is thor oughly r eli abl e and loyal. Performance of his collateral
duty has been uniformly efficient. His personal and military character•
istics are excel lent,and he i s recommended for retention of present
temporary r ank and for promotion when eligible.
Exhibit "F"
Rear Admiral C.W.Fox,SC,USN 22 October 1947 Cdr.H.J .P.Foley,Jr.,SC,USN
12. •satisfactory."
Lt. Newhouse 1s performance of duty has conti nued to be excellent and
outstanding• . I n additi on to a sound grasp of area and service-wide
photographic supply procedures, he possesses mature judgment and a
high order of devotion t o duty. He is willing to accept responsibility
and compl et es assignments with interest and dispatch. His personal and
mi litary character i s excellent,and he has been r ecommended for a

l. .Your application for transfer to commissioned 'limited duty status CC) has been _carefully considered by the Selection Board convened by the
Secretary of the Navy. I regret to inform you that your name wae not
includod among those recommended for appointment. However, I wish to
assure you that this decision is no reflection upon your perfor~ce
o! duty in the Navy; nor vill it in any way affect your .present status
or preclude your making application and being considered by subsequent
selection boards.
2. The number of applicants desiring transfer to commissioned limited
duty statue has been far in excess of the number of appointments which
can be offered. Therefore it was necessary for the Selection Board
to recommend, , from among eiigible a~~licante, only those best qualified
for appointment. The decision in your case was made after a careful
review of your entire record and your qualifications ao compared with
those ot other applicants of your rRnk and classification or enlisted
rating.
3. The interest you have shown in pursuing a naval career represents
an exemplary attitude of serVice and patriotism. The Navy ie sincerely
appreciative of your contribution toward maintaining the naval estab￾lishment at a high level of efficiency during the war, the demobiliza￾tion period, and the continuing period of world unrest.
T. L. SPRAGUE

EXPLANATION OF SCORES
I. The scores are given in percentiles. The percentile sc;res r un from 01 to 99 and
indicate the percent of candidates receiving a lowe-r score. Thus, if a candidate
has a percentile score 63, 63 percent of the candidates taking the test made a
lower score and 37 percent had the same or a higher score.
2. It sho uld be _noted that the total score percentile is rrot a n average of the
percentiles for the sections, but indicates a candidate's relative standfog on the
basis of his total test score. Candidates with a generaJ.ly high r ecord on the test
will have a _percentile on the tobal score that is higher than the average of thek
percentiles on the sections. Simila rly, candid.ates with a generally low. record
on the. test will have a lower percentile on the total score than the average of
their section percentile scores.

INFORMATION AND INSTRUCTIONS CONCERNING THIS ELECTION FORM
(For Nonretired members)
1. It is desired that all members with 17 years service creditable for basic pay ·purposes, active or inactive, either decline or
elect to provide certain annuities for their dependents under the provisions of the Uniformed Services Contingency Option Act of
1953 (Public Law 239-83rd. Congress). If you desire to receive a reduction in the retired pay which you may subsequently receive,
thereby providing an annuity for your dependents, you must elect the desired option (s) not later than 30 Apri l 1954 or prior
to completion of 18 years service c reditable for basic pay purposes, whichever is later. The terms of the election may be modi￾fied or revoked prior to retirement, but any modification or revocation will not be effective if you retire within five years from
the date of such modification or revocation.
2. If you do not desire to receive reduced retired pay to provide an annuity for your dependents, check the square opposite
"I do not desire to receive reduced retired pay in order to provide an annuity for my dependents" on the face of this form,
sign all copies of the form and return it to the Bureau of Naval Personnel, Washington 25, D. C. You may subsequently elect
to be covered by this Act, provided you submit such election to the Bureau of Naval Personnel, Washington 25, D. C., ,!!2!
later than 30 April 1954 or prior to completion of 18 yc:irs service creditable for basic pay purposes. whichever is later.
3. If you desire to receive reduced retired pay to provide an annuity for your dependents, fill out the form completely check￾ing desired option (s) ; a ll copies must be signed and notarized or attested.
4. If either Option 2 or 3 is elected, it is required that all eligible children be named.-
5. If should be noted that, under Option 3, all benefits are paid to the widow until her death or remarriage and thereafter
in equal sha res to or on behalf of the surviving children remaining eligible at the time the payment is due. Consequently, if it
is desired that children of a former marriage receive payments concurrently with the widow, Options l and 2 should be elected.
6. In indicating the place of dissolution of a former marriage, please include the city, county and state.
7. The triplicate of this form is to be retained by you for your personal file. For active duty personnel, the original will be
forwarded to the Bureau of Nava l Personnel, W ashington 25, D. C., and the duplicate is to be filed in your personnel jacket at
your station of duty. For members in the reserve components, the original and duplicate will be forwa rded to the Bureau of
Naval Personnel, W ashington 25, D. C.

equal shares.

1. Commander Naval Air Force, U.S. Atlantic Fleet takes pleasure j_n
certifying the Operations Department of the USS ESSEX (CVS-9) as standing
first in the CVA class ship during fiscal year 1960.
2. The Commanding Officer is congratulated on the exemplary perform.auce
of the ESSEX's Operations Department .
3. The ESSEX is authorized to display the Operations Department Excellence
Insignia in accordance with reference (a).

and men of the ESSEX who materially contributed to the -earning of this
award. The Commanding Officer is requested to forward a copy of this
letter to the command of those persom1el formerly serving on board during
fiscal year 1960, who significantly contributed to the winning of this
award.

Subj: Commendat ion f or Operations performed-during Mediterranean and WesPac Deployment
1. The Commanding Officer is in receipt of many complimentary
communications -concerning t he out standing performance of ESSEX
and Air Task Group 201 during t he period 2 February to
17 November 1958.
2. During this period ESSEX and her emba rked Air Group served
as a striking unit of the Sixt h Fleet in Mediterranean waters,
and her aircraf t provided i ni t i al and sustained air support
for U. 3. Marine landings i n Lebanon from 15 July to August
1958. Th~se operations, under wartime conditions, were per￾formed by the men of ESSEX and ATG 201 with an outstanding
degree of professional skill a nd devotion to duty.
J. Following this, ESSEX was deployed on an emergency basis
to t he Far East , where a ir operations in support of U.S.
~at ional policy during the Formosa crisis were conducted in
the same outstanding manner .

are the following ·excerpt s from various communications·

cwoh NE'WHOUSE has performed and carried out his duti es as Assistant Fl eet Air Photo- graphic Officer in an excellent manner. His devotion to duty as a naval officer in the
field of photography sets an example for personnel in his specialty to follow. He
maintains and generates a high degree of morale among the men with whom he works. His
past exoerience and excellent knowl edge of photogr aphy has been a great help both to
the oer sonnel and to the efficient oper ation of the Lab. He has a pleasant and radiant
personality.
